(CMI), a small company founded in 1990 in San Antonio, Texas. CMI was originally established by Elaine Mendoza and built credibility through Department of Defense software contracts (US Air Force, Army, Navy) before expanding into healthcare IT. Sevocity launched its first live customer in September 2002, achieved CCHIT certification in 2008, Meaningful Use certification in 2011, and ONC 2015 Edition Cures certification for v13.0 in December 2022.\n     9→\n    10→Sevocity is a small vendor by industry standards. Third-party data (GetLatka) reports approximately $2.9M in revenue in 2024 (up from $2.1M in 2023) with about 18 total employees. The product was named in Capterra's Top 20 Most Popular EMR software. It targets solo practitioners and small-to-mid-size ambulatory practices across the United States, having expanded from 10 states in 2009 to 40+ states by 2014. The product emphasizes customization — it supports 40+ medical specialties with pre-built specialty content that can be further customized per practice and provider. There is no evidence of hospital, inpatient, or enterprise deployments; this is firmly an outpatient/ambulatory system.\n    11→\n    12→## Product: Sevocity\n    13→\n    14→CHPL ID: 11187 (Sevocity v13.0, certified 2022-12-30)\n    15→\n    16→### What It Is\n    17→\n    18→Sevocity is a cloud-based electronic health records (EHR) system for ambulatory medical practices. The certified product appears to be the complete EHR system itself — there is no indication that the certified module is a subset of a larger platform. However, Sevocity offers two product tiers:\n    19→\n    20→1. **Sevocity EHR** — the core electronic health records system, which can operate standalone or integrate with third-party practice management/billing systems via HL7 interfaces.\n    21→2. **Sevocity Premier** — an all-in-one bundle that adds integrated practice management and billing capabilities (including a fully integrated clearinghouse, unlimited claims, and Electronic Remittance Advices) to the EHR. This was announced in a 2018 press release.\n    22→\n    23→The CHPL certification covers \"Sevocity\" broadly, and the certified criteria span clinical documentation, e-prescribing, clinical decision support, patient portal, FHIR APIs, transitions of care, and public health reporting — indicating the certified module encompasses the full clinical EHR.\n    24→\n    25→### Users & Market\n    26→\n    27→**Target users**: Physicians, nurse practitioners, and clinical staff in outpatient/ambulatory settings. The SED intended user description is \"Outpatient Clinic.\" Practice managers and billing staff are also mentioned as users, particularly for Sevocity Premier.\n    28→\n    29→**Practice sizes**: Solo practices through groups of 50+ physicians, though the product is primarily marketed to solo and small group practices.\n    30→\n    31→**Specialties**: 40+ specialties supported, including family medicine, internal medicine, pediatrics, OB/GYN, cardiology, dermatology, orthopedics, gastroenterology, neurology, psychiatry, mental health, substance abuse, urgent care, physical therapy, pain management, urology, general surgery, plastic surgery, geriatrics, endocrinology, pulmonology, rheumatology, nephrology, otolaryngology, and more.\n    32→\n    33→**Settings**: Community health centers (FQHCs are specifically mentioned in marketing), primary care practices, specialty practices. No hospital or inpatient use.\n    34→\n    35→**Customer count**: Not disclosed on the website. Revenue data suggests a small customer base consistent with ~$2.9M annual revenue and pricing at ~$379/provider/month.\n    36→\n    37→### Modules & Functionality\n    38→\n    39→Based on vendor materials, the knowledge base, third-party review sites, and the CHPL certification criteria, Sevocity includes the following modules and capabilities:\n    40→\n    41→**Clinical Documentation & Charting**\n    42→- Customizable encounter templates per specialty and provider (vendor knowledge base, specialties page)\n    43→- Physical exam documentation (EMRSystems review)\n    44→- Flexible flow sheets (EHRinPractice profile)\n    45→- E&M coding calculator for evaluation and management level determination (multiple review sites)\n    46→- Voice recognition support (EHRinPractice profile)\n    47→- ICD-10 coding with customizable favorites lists and master search (FAQ page)\n    48→- CPT/HCPCS coding for procedures and orders (knowledge base)\n    49→\n    50→**Orders, Labs & Results**\n    51→- Lab and diagnostic test ordering with CPT, HCPCS, or SNOMED coding (knowledge base Orders-Referrals page)\n    52→- Electronic lab ordering via Quest bi-directional interface (knowledge base)\n    53→- Lab results delivered to clinic inbox (knowledge base)\n    54→- Specimen collection details (specimen type, dates, times) tracked (knowledge base)\n    55→- Pre-built interfaces with most major labs (FAQ page)\n    56→- In-house lab specimen label printing (knowledge base)\n    57→\n    58→**E-Prescribing**\n    59→- Certified embedded e-prescribing including EPCS documented in Results section (knowledge base search results)\n    72→\n    73→**Practice Management & Scheduling**\n    74→- Appointment scheduling with multiple calendar views (day, week, month) (CMI), a small company founded in 1990 in San Antonio, Texas. CMI was originally established by Elaine Mendoza and built credibility through Department of Defense software contracts (US Air Force, Army, Navy) before expanding into healthcare IT. Sevocity launched its first live customer in September 2002, achieved CCHIT certification in 2008, Meaningful Use certification in 2011, and ONC 2015 Edition Cures certification for v13.0 in December 2022.\n\nSevocity is a small vendor by industry standards. Third-party data (GetLatka) reports approximately $2.9M in revenue in 2024 (up from $2.1M in 2023) with about 18 total employees. The product was named in Capterra's Top 20 Most Popular EMR software. It targets solo practitioners and small-to-mid-size ambulatory practices across the United States, having expanded from 10 states in 2009 to 40+ states by 2014. The product emphasizes customization — it supports 40+ medical specialties with pre-built specialty content that can be further customized per practice and provider. There is no evidence of hospital, inpatient, or enterprise deployments; this is firmly an outpatient/ambulatory system.\n\n## Product: Sevocity\n\nCHPL ID: 11187 (Sevocity v13.0, certified 2022-12-30)\n\n### What It Is\n\nSevocity is a cloud-based electronic health records (EHR) system for ambulatory medical practices. The certified product appears to be the complete EHR system itself — there is no indication that the certified module is a subset of a larger platform. However, Sevocity offers two product tiers:\n\n1. **Sevocity EHR** — the core electronic health records system, which can operate standalone or integrate with third-party practice management/billing systems via HL7 interfaces.\n2. **Sevocity Premier** — an all-in-one bundle that adds integrated practice management and billing capabilities (including a fully integrated clearinghouse, unlimited claims, and Electronic Remittance Advices) to the EHR. This was announced in a 2018 press release.\n\nThe CHPL certification covers \"Sevocity\" broadly, and the certified criteria span clinical documentation, e-prescribing, clinical decision support, patient portal, FHIR APIs, transitions of care, and public health reporting — indicating the certified module encompasses the full clinical EHR.\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Target users**: Physicians, nurse practitioners, and clinical staff in outpatient/ambulatory settings. The SED intended user description is \"Outpatient Clinic.\" Practice managers and billing staff are also mentioned as users, particularly for Sevocity Premier.\n\n**Practice sizes**: Solo practices through groups of 50+ physicians, though the product is primarily marketed to solo and small group practices.\n\n**Specialties**: 40+ specialties supported, including family medicine, internal medicine, pediatrics, OB/GYN, cardiology, dermatology, orthopedics, gastroenterology, neurology, psychiatry, mental health, substance abuse, urgent care, physical therapy, pain management, urology, general surgery, plastic surgery, geriatrics, endocrinology, pulmonology, rheumatology, nephrology, otolaryngology, and more.\n\n**Settings**: Community health centers (FQHCs are specifically mentioned in marketing), primary care practices, specialty practices. No hospital or inpatient use.\n\n**Customer count**: Not disclosed on the website. Revenue data suggests a small customer base consistent with ~$2.9M annual revenue and pricing at ~$379/provider/month.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor materials, the knowledge base, third-party review sites, and the CHPL certification criteria, Sevocity includes the following modules and capabilities:\n\n**Clinical Documentation & Charting**\n- Customizable encounter templates per specialty and provider (vendor knowledge base, specialties page)\n- Physical exam documentation (EMRSystems review)\n- Flexible flow sheets (EHRinPractice profile)\n- E&M coding calculator for evaluation and management level determination (multiple review sites)\n- Voice recognition support (EHRinPractice profile)\n- ICD-10 coding with customizable favorites lists and master search (FAQ page)\n- CPT/HCPCS coding for procedures and orders (knowledge base)\n\n**Orders, Labs & Results**\n- Lab and diagnostic test ordering with CPT, HCPCS, or SNOMED coding (knowledge base Orders-Referrals page)\n- Electronic lab ordering via Quest bi-directional interface (knowledge base)\n- Lab results delivered to clinic inbox (knowledge base)\n- Specimen collection details (specimen type, dates, times) tracked (knowledge base)\n- Pre-built interfaces with most major labs (FAQ page)\n- In-house lab specimen label printing (knowledge base)\n\n**E-Prescribing**\n- Certified embedded e-prescribing including EPCS (Electronic Prescribing of Controlled Substances) (specialties page, Capterra page)\n- Drug interaction alerts (search results from EMRFinder)\n- Integration with Surescripts implied by e-prescribing certification ((a)(4) certified)\n\n**Patient Portal**\n- Real-time patient portal for direct patient-physician communication (multiple sources)\n- Patient access to health information (certified for (e)(1) view/download/transmit)\n- Patient intake and data collection features (EMRSystems review)\n\n**Referral Management**\n- Provider referrals with customizable specialties and contacts (knowledge base)\n- Electronic referral sending via Direct protocol (knowledge base)\n- Referral reports from specialists documented in Results section (knowledge base search results)\n\n**Practice Management & Scheduling**\n- Appointment scheduling with multiple calendar views (day, week, month) (search results)\n- Demographics management (multiple sources)\n- Multi-office capability (EHRinPractice profile)\n\n**Billing & Claims** (Sevocity Premier tier)\n- Integrated clearinghouse with unlimited claims submission (Capterra page, Premier announcement)\n- Claim status dashboards (Capterra page)\n- Electronic Remittance Advices (ERAs) (Premier announcement)\n- Eligibility verification (Capterra page)\n- Statement processing and distribution (search results)\n- For non-Premier customers: bi-directional HL7 integration with 12 partner PM/billing systems including AMS Software, BrickMed, DuxWare, EZClaim, PracticeAdmin, PracticeSuite, Puredi, and others (Partners page)\n\n**Document Management**\n- File/document storage with individual files up to 4MB (expandable to 20MB for a fee), unlimited total storage (system requirements search)\n- ID/insurance card scanning (system requirements search)\n- E-faxing (multiple sources)\n\n**Immunizations**\n- Immunization tracking with 2D barcode scanner support (system requirements search)\n- State immunization registry interfaces (system requirements search)\n- Certified for immunization registry reporting ((f)(1))\n\n**Public Health Reporting**\n- Immunization registry reporting — certified (f)(1)\n- Syndromic surveillance reporting — certified (f)(2)\n\n**Clinical Decision Support**\n- Clinical decision support interventions — certified (a)(2)\n- Standardized treatment protocols (search results)\n\n**Transitions of Care**\n- C-CDA generation and consumption — certified (b)(1) and (b)(2)\n- Direct messaging for care coordination\n\n**FHIR/API Access**\n- FHIR API for patient and population services — certified (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10)\n\n**Reporting & Analytics**\n- Clinical quality measures / MIPS reporting — certified (c)(1), (c)(2), (c)(3)\n- Reporting and analytics capabilities (EHRinPractice profile)\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the features described above, Sevocity stores the following categories of data:\n\n- **Patient demographics** — });\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Parse path files\n  const pathsDir = join(BASE_DIR, \"paths\");\n  if (existsSync(pathsDir)) {\n    const pathFiles = readdirSync(pathsDir).filter((f) => Zero failures.\n\n### Step 6: Screenshots\n\nTook full-page screenshots of both the main EHI export documentation page and the Swagger UI FHIR API docs page using browser automation.\n\n## What Was Found\n\nSevocity's EHI export documentation consists of two components:\n\n### 1. EHI Export Format Description (main page)\n\nThe main page at the registered URL describes the structure of the EHI export package. The export is delivered as an **encrypted RAR file** with the following folder structure:\n\n| Path | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `/{Customer ID}/README.txt` | Contains the documentation URL (points back to this page) |\n| `/{Customer ID}/DocumentData/{Patient ID}/` | All document and image data (PDFs, JPEGs, PNGs). Contains `summary.pdf` (chart summary) plus subfolders for document types: \"admissions\", \"finalizedencounters\", \"referrals\", etc. |\n| `/{Customer ID}/Resources/` | FHIR resources in NDJSON format. DocumentReference resources with `type.coding.code` of \"11503-0\" have `content.attachment.url` pointing to files in the DocumentData folder. |\n| `/{Customer ID}/PatientsDemographics.csv` | CSV file with all patient demographics |\n\nThe Patient ID is an internal value; the PatientsDemographics.csv file maps patients to their IDs.\n\n### 2. Sevocity FHIR API Documentation (Swagger UI)\n\nThe FHIR API docs at `fhirapi-docs.sevocity.com` are a comprehensive OpenAPI 3.0.1 specification documenting 25 FHIR resource types with:\n\n- **Read and Search operations** for each resource type\n- **Detailed JSON schemas** for every resource (read variant and search/Bundle variant)\n- **Example instances** for all 25 resource types, with realistic sample data\n- **Group/$export endpoint** for FHIR Bulk Data export\n- **CapabilityStatement** (metadata) example showing all supported resources\n\nThe API documentation explicitly distinguishes certified (g)(10) resources (marked with `*`) from non-certified resources. The API serves FHIR R4 resources conformant to US Core STU6.1.0.\n\n**FHIR Resource Types in the Export:**\n\nCertified (g)(10) — 19 resource types:\nAllergyIntolerance, CarePlan, CareTeam, Condition, Coverage, Device, DiagnosticReport, DocumentReference, Encounter, Endpoint, Goal, Immunization, Location, Medication, MedicationDispense, MedicationRequest, Observation, Organization, Patient, Practitioner, Procedure, Provenance\n\nNon-certified additions — 6 resource types:\nAppointment, MedicationStatement, Questionnaire, QuestionnaireResponse, RelatedPerson, ServiceRequest\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe EHI export uses a hybrid approach: FHIR NDJSON resources plus a file-based document export with a CSV demographics file. This is genuinely more than just a (g)(10) FHIR API repackage — Sevocity has done real work to go beyond the standard API:\n\n**Clearly covered:**\n- **Demographics** — Patient resource (64 fields) with US Core extensions for race, ethnicity, birthsex, sex, gender identity, and tribal affiliation. Plus a CSV demographics file.\n- **Problems/conditions** — Condition resource with ICD-10-CM coding\n- **Medications** — MedicationRequest, MedicationStatement, MedicationDispense, and Medication resources (covering prescriptions, active meds, dispensing)\n- **Allergies** — AllergyIntolerance resource\n- **Lab results** — DiagnosticReport and Observation resources\n- **Vital signs** — Observation resource (covers vitals via LOINC codes)\n- **Immunizations** — Immunization resource\n- **Procedures** — Procedure resource\n- **Encounters/visits** — Encounter resource (45 fields including diagnoses, participants, locations)\n- **Care plans and goals** — CarePlan and Goal resources\n- **Care teams** — CareTeam resource\n- **Referrals/orders** — ServiceRequest resource (non-certified addition covering lab and diagnostic orders with CPT/HCPCS/SNOMED coding and ICD-10-CM reason codes)\n- **Insurance/coverage** — Coverage resource with member numbers, payer references, plan details\n- **Documents/images** — DocumentReference resources plus raw files (PDFs, JPEGs, PNGs) in DocumentData folders, organized by patient with subfolders for document types\n- **Clinical assessments** — QuestionnaireResponse resource — the example shows a Zung Self-Rating Depression Scale with 20 scored items, indicating Sevocity exports structured screening tool responses\n- **Questionnaires** — Questionnaire resource providing the templates/definitions for assessments\n- **Appointments** — Appointment resource (non-certified addition)\n- **Related persons/contacts** — RelatedPerson resource for emergency contacts and guarantors\n- **Provenance** — Provenance resource for data provenance tracking\n- **Organizations** — Organization resource\n- **Practitioners** — Practitioner resource\n- **Locations** — Location resource\n\n**Notable strengths beyond (g)(10):**\n- The `DocumentData/` folder structure exports raw documents (PDFs, images) organized by patient, including chart summaries, finalized encounters, admissions documents, and referrals — this is data that FHIR alone cannot fully represent\n- QuestionnaireResponse captures structured clinical assessments (depression scales, etc.) which are specialty-specific clinical data that most FHIR-only exports miss\n- Appointment and ServiceRequest are non-certified resources that add scheduling and order data\n- MedicationStatement adds historical medication use beyond just prescriptions\n\n**Potentially missing or unclear:**\n- **Billing data** — Coverage (insurance) is present, but there are no Claim, ExplanationOfBenefit, or ChargeItem resources. Sevocity Premier includes integrated billing, so for those customers, detailed billing records (claims, charges, payments, ERAs) may not be fully captured in the FHIR export. The documentation does not mention billing exports beyond insurance coverage.\n- **Clinical notes/narrative text** — The DocumentData folder likely contains these as PDFs, but the FHIR resources don't include a specific note-text resource. Clinical notes may be embedded in DocumentReference resources or in the `summary.pdf` files.\n- **E-prescribing details** — While MedicationRequest captures prescriptions, EPCS-specific data (controlled substance schedules, digital signatures) isn't explicitly documented.\n- **PatientsDemographics.csv schema** — The CSV file is mentioned but its exact column structure is not documented. Only described as \"all patient demographics.\"\n- **Custom specialty template data** — Sevocity supports 40+ specialties with customizable templates. It's unclear whether specialty-specific charting fields (beyond what QuestionnaireResponse covers) are fully captured.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a thoughtful hybrid format:\n\n1. **FHIR R4 NDJSON** — Machine-readable structured clinical data conformant to US Core STU6.1.0 profiles. Resources are in the `Resources/` folder.\n2. **Raw document files** — PDFs, images (JPEG, PNG) in `DocumentData/` organized by patient ID and document type. This is critical because many clinical documents (scanned records, chart summaries, referral letters) cannot be adequately represented as pure FHIR resources.\n3. **CSV demographics file** — Maps patients to internal IDs and provides demographic data in a simple tabular format.\n4. **Encrypted RAR archive** — The entire export is delivered as an encrypted RAR file, providing transport-level security for PHI.\n\nThe FHIR resources use standard code systems (ICD-10-CM, SNOMED CT, LOINC, CPT, HCPCS, RxNorm via NDC) and reference each other via standard FHIR references. DocumentReference resources with LOINC code \"11503-0\" (Medical records) link to the physical files in DocumentData via relative URLs.\n\nA third party could reconstruct a patient's clinical record from this export, given the FHIR resources for structured data and the document files for unstructured content. The PatientsDemographics.csv bridges the internal patient IDs used in folder names to patient identifiers.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The OpenAPI spec is comprehensive — 25 resource types with detailed JSON schemas, search parameters, and example instances for every resource\n- Examples use realistic test data (San Antonio addresses, clinical codes, structured assessment responses)\n- The Swagger UI presentation is interactive and navigable\n- The main EHI export page clearly explains the RAR file structure and how FHIR resources relate to document files\n- Clear distinction between (g)(10) certified and additional resources\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- The PatientsDemographics.csv file format is not documented — no column headers, data types, or example provided\n- No documentation of the NDJSON file naming conventions or how many NDJSON files are in the Resources folder\n- No user guide for requesting or performing the export — no instructions on how to trigger the export, who can request it, or the expected turnaround time\n- The main documentation page is minimal (one table) — the heavy lifting is delegated to the FHIR API docs\n- No description of how the export handles large datasets, pagination, or incremental exports\n- The \"encrypted rar file\" delivery mechanism lacks details — encryption algorithm, key distribution, file size limits\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**Schema granularity:** Field-level documentation is strong. The OpenAPI schemas define every field with JSON types. The Patient resource alone has 64 nested fields including US Core extensions. However, the schemas are structural (type information) without semantic descriptions — fields like `status` are typed as `string` but don't enumerate valid values.\n\n**Value sets:** Not explicitly documented. Coded fields (e.g., Condition.code, AllergyIntolerance.clinicalStatus) use `type: string` without binding to FHIR value sets. However, the examples demonstrate use of standard code systems (ICD-10-CM, SNOMED CT, LOINC), so practitioners familiar with FHIR US Core would understand the expected value sets.\n\n**Relationships:** FHIR references between resources are documented in the schemas (e.g., Encounter.subject.reference → Patient, ServiceRequest.encounter.reference → Encounter). The DocumentReference→DocumentData linkage via LOINC code 11503-0 is explicitly called out on the main page.\n\n**Versioning:** The OpenAPI spec lists version 1.0.0 with US Core STU6.1.0 as the external docs reference. The main page was last modified 2024-05-14 (per page metadata). No formal change history.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://www.sevocity.com/ehi-export-technical-documentation/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl; Swagger UI for interactive browsing but underlying YAML files are directly downloadable)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (main page → FHIR API docs link)\n- Anti-bot issues: none (Cloudflare present but did not block automated access)\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\nNone.
